Edge cases (edge_tests): a peak before the daily record began = skipped, the next largest used; peaks coded historic, daily mean, dam failure, lower bound or unknown date = not used; under 10 of the 14 days published = skipped; the instantaneous peak is higher than any daily mean (said); lakes, coasts, world = none; two big peaks in one flood (within 14 days) = one flood.
   (d, original) Past-flood pages: a gauge's 3 largest peaks of record, each with the gauge's recorded rise and fall from the daily record, only where the daily record covers it; not built where under 7 days around the peak are published.
   (e) OWNER (go-ahead per batch, feedback_outreach-signing): research pitches, findings read from the live CSVs 2026-11-12. NOT pitched: single-gauge peak-change results (3 up, 1 down of 87 at p < 0.05, while chance alone gives about 2.2 each way: no field-wide signal, so a headline on one gauge would mislead). Pitch 1, Dallas outlets (Dallas Morning News, KERA): 'The Trinity at Dallas reaches NWS minor flood stage (30 ft) in most years: 80 of 92 water years in the usable USGS record, median yearly peak stage 35.9 ft (USGS peaks since 1904, read live)'. Pitch 2, San Antonio outlets (Express-News, Texas Public Radio): 'Medina Lake 27% and Choke Canyon 21% of conservation capacity, against Canyon Lake 97% and Lake Travis 100% (TWDB daily, Nov 12)'. Pitch 3, Las Vegas Review-Journal: 'Lake Mead 26.8% of capacity to 1,220 ft (Reclamation daily table)'. Each pitch links the research page and its CSV, signed Michael, reply to contact@. On the owner's go: find each outlet's published tips address, send, record in data/outreach.csv.
   (e, original) Outreach for the research pages: local newsrooms and state agencies named per finding; drafts for the owner's go-ahead per batch (feedback_outreach-signing).
   (f) DECIDED 2026-11-12, not built: the home table already ranks every gauge against normal today and the research pages rebuild with the data; a dated weekly archive adds pages more than answers (three tests: helpful fails). Original note: Weekly roundup page, dated and archived: furthest from normal (rivers, lakes, coasts), from the same research functions.
13. WAITING 2026-11-19 (code on check, 2026-11-12) 'What happened next in years like this one' (owner: something original like slabcalc). Years whose daily mean on today's date was within 25% of today's flow, and their flow 7 days later: counts higher / about the same / lower, median change, one row per year (lib/charts.py b_like_today, after the river forecast; collect.py next_days stores 21 days a year from the full daily record already fetched weekly: no new call). Helpful: most gauges say 'Not issued' for a river forecast; this answers 'what usually comes next' from the gauge's own record / Gain: no agency page shows it / HCU: our own calculation, labelled history, not a forecast. Real data, Helen 02330450: 11 of 45 years, higher in 7, same 3, lower 1, median +16%. Scale: about 21 x 45 numbers a gauge in the record cache; nothing per visit. WAITING 2026-11-19: the block appears as each gauge's weekly history fetch (lib/cadence.py WEEKLY multiyear) next runs, all within 7 days; then read /river/chattahoochee-river/helen/ live and run the phone review (new block type): gh workflow run dryrun.yml --ref check -f review="02330450".
   Edge cases (tools/edge_tests.py, all pass): this year never counted; Feb 29 start; a year under half its window dropped; no year within 25% = no block; under 5 matches or 10 years = no block; build more than 13 days after the weekly fetch = no block; lake, tide, stage-only, offline or no flow = no block; 'about the same' uses the page's own 2% / 5 cfs rule.


## Trimmed 2026-11-27 (whole PLAN.md as it was)

# PLAN: riverwaterlevels.com

History (dated batch logs, audits, debates, research, old step lists): `docs/plan-history.md`. Rules: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Goal
OBJECTIVE ABOVE ALL (owner 2026-11-20): make as much money as possible. Release 25 pages a day until the queue is empty (owner 2026-11-20); no keyword or water is dropped for being small. The most useful page for a person deciding about this river today, anywhere (reader: CLAUDE.md), is how the money is earned. AdSense earnings from people helped.
Scarce resource: Claude time. From 2026-11-20 the owner moves from Max to Pro (owner 2026-11-03): sessions continue but far fewer, so growth slows, never stops; everything must run alone between sessions, and the costly builds (world release, limits) belong before Nov 20.
Bottleneck: AdSense approval, then readers. Earns $0 until approved; calcscrape and asmrregistry were rejected as low value content, so approval outranks page count.

## DECIDED 2026-11-20: why traffic is flat, and what we do (Claude and ChatGPT agreed, owner debate)
Evidence (GSC 28 d, read 2026-11-20): 35 clicks, 4,130 impressions, position 24.1, CTR 0.8%; 417 of 572 inspected pages indexed (data/index-inspect.json); live check: 122 of 129 pages listed noindex in GSC are indexable now (the report is stale); 563 of 746 queries rank beyond 40, 568 have 1 impression. Only repeated cluster: Rio Grande at Albuquerque (7 phrasings, ~105 impressions, position 6.7-9). "How deep is the [river] in [town]" appears for 5 rivers (position 5-44); our pages already carry the depth block (lib/blocks3.py:440) but not near the top. Tide queries 182 impressions at position 73. Keyword Planner total ~930k searches a month, so realistic near-term revenue is tens to a few hundred USD a month; $1,500 is not reachable from this keyword set (ASSUMED RPM $5).
Agreed list: (1) no global atlas, no Argo, glider, satellite or model layers, no new data sources (fail reader and thin-page tests); (2) no full Keyword Planner rerun; use GSC, autosuggest, People Also Ask and a 40-river SERP audit; (3) depth line near the top of gauge pages that already have the depth block, worded "at a flow similar to today's" (not "today's flow"), test on 5 pages for 28 days (Ohio Louisville, Colorado Austin, Missouri Omaha, Mississippi St Louis; Danube hub unchecked); (4) Albuquerque: leave the title alone (TITLE_TODAY test, lib/page.py:95), improve H1 and first sentence, read the top-10 SERP first; (5) status sentence carries number, unit and time (lib/hero.py, likely; edge_tests.py:1365 and :1376 assert the old wording); (6) keep releasing ~15 pages a day behind a quality gate (real gauge, attributable data, own geographic identity); (7) keep tide pages indexed, fix missing `license` on 13 coast pages; (8) links: outreach to paddling and fishing guides, watershed groups, university hydrology, local newsrooms, municipal riverfront pages, using embed widgets and the water-temperature dataset page; (9) measure ChatGPT referrals by engaged sessions and landing pages, not volume. Unresolved nuance: ChatGPT says relevance, not only links, can move individual pages (Albuquerque shows it); Claude accepts: links for the long tail, relevance for the ~30 queries at positions 3-20.
Edge cases: depth line on a gauge with no depth measurement shows nothing (test: fixture); a stale measurement date must show its age; title test not spoiled (test: Albuquerque title unchanged). STATUS: not built; code needs /code-review medium before push.
Seasonality + focus list (Keyword Planner file data/kwp/results-2026-11-13.csv, three-month change column, read 2026-11-20): tubing -84%, kayaking -61%, swimming -47% (tier-weighted), fishing report +60%; so November is the trough for paddling and tubing and current traffic undercounts summer (peak size not measured; Google Trends not yet checked). Top waters by activity searches (tubing, kayaking, swimming, fishing report, boat ramp, tier midpoints): Salt River 56,050 (15 gauge pages in pages-all), Delaware 11,500 (25), Madison 10,050 (8), Green 7,000 (32), Russian 6,500 (10), Guadalupe 6,100 (19), San Marcos 6,050 (3), Chattahoochee 5,650 (19). Next: make the ~50 highest-demand recreation waters the best result before April 2027 (page answers, links, outfitter embeds), instead of spreading effort over every gauge. Counts of pages are queued plus live and name-matched, so 'white river' and 'elm creek' style names are unreliable.

DECIDED 2026-11-21 (Claude and ChatGPT agreed): prevent release stalls and health noise. Cause of the 11-19 and 11-20 stalls: truth_tests.py flagged '14th Street' on rivers/ga; only check.py failures are attributable (dist_next/_failed.json), so tools/quarantine.py undid all 25 picks. Design: (1) every gate failure carries a scope (gauge, batch, source, site) and an unattributed failure is UNKNOWN_SCOPE, never 'drop all 25'; (2) release is transactional: staging, manifest, queue changes only after a successful deploy; (3) a failed batch shrinks (25, 12, 6, 3, 1), commits what passes, returns failing gauges to the queue with a reason; a site-wide fault stops retries; (4) offline preflight of all ~7,191 queued gauges (names and would-be page text) against the text rules, nightly and on every rule change; (5) source lifecycle ACTIVE, DEGRADED, DISABLED (confirmed deterministic failures such as the HTTP 400 floodfreq and never-worked itis, retested weekly) so dead sources leave the active health list; (6) the metric that drives action is new live pages published in the last 24 h: at 12 h try a smaller batch, at 24 h recovery mode, the hourly watchdog may start the recovery; optional feeds (NWM, flood frequency, weather) fail open with 'unavailable', core readings and identity fail closed. Order and estimate (ChatGPT, guess): scope attribution 25 min, '14th Street' fixture 20 (done: ORD_JARGON in truth_tests.py), batch isolation and smaller retry 40, full-queue preflight 30, source-disable states 25, 24 h watchdog action 20. Tests to add: one invalid gauge cannot cancel 24; unknown scope cannot roll back the batch; a failed batch eventually publishes its passing gauges; a failed deploy loses no queue entries. STATUS: fixture done, rest not built; each change needs /code-review before push.

- NEXT BUILD 2026-11-24 LIVE-WATER MAP (the destination page; owner 'Ok' to ChatGPT's order; NOT started). THREE TESTS: Helpful: one screen where a person finds their water, sees the latest reading and its age at a glance, and opens the page; Gain: USGS/TWDB/lakeinsights give one water per page, none gives every water on one map with ages and units; HCU: an interactive tool is main content, not a doorway, and links only to built indexable pages. DESIGN (cheapest rung first): one static page /map/ (breadcrumb home > map, depth 2, linked from the home page and the footer), an inline SVG world outline (Natural Earth 110m, public domain, ~100 KB; download in the cloud, never commit a big file) plus one dot per LIVE page from the build's own cache (name, lat, lon, latest value + unit, observed time, state: live/stale/unknown, url); no tile server, no third-party script, no key, no cookies; JS = pan/zoom by viewBox, search box (name), filter by water type and country, tap a dot -> card with reading, age, source, 'open page'. Stale readings greyed with the real age; Unknown never drawn as safe; numbers come only from the page's own rendered reading (one source of truth, so map and page cannot disagree: a build check compares them). Size cap: MAX_GZ and one JSON of about 520 -> 8,000 dots (about 60 bytes each = 480 KB raw at full scale: split by region before 2,000 dots). Phone first: dots >= 24 px tap area, list view under the map for screen readers. Needs: `lib/` new page kind registered in check.py kinds and the doorway/orphan/depth rules, REVIEW_BLOCKS + data/reviewed.json phone shots, sitemap entry, a fixture. EDGE CASES (written before code): (1) a dot whose page is later held or deleted links to a 404: build only from the pages in the same build; (2) lat/lon swapped or zero (0,0) puts a lake in the ocean: drop and log any dot outside its country's box, check in the build; (3) one reading unit mix (ft vs cfs vs % full) on one map: show the unit on every card, never a colour scale across different measures; (4) 8,000 dots on a phone: cluster or regional split, measure INP; (5) a stale feed shows an old number as current: age words on the card, grey after STALE_H; (6) Natural Earth borders disputes: use the neutral outline, no border labels; (7) the map becomes a doorway: it must carry its own text (what the colours mean, how fresh, where data comes from) and each dot opens a different page; (8) AdSense: no ads inside the map; the page itself is the reviewer-visible proof of a tool. Order after the map: history charts on existing pages (24 h / 7 d / 30 d, only from data already cached), saved favourites in localStorage (no sign-in, try/catch), alerts last. OWNER: none needed.

- DECIDED 2026-11-22 KEYWORD PLANNER STRATEGY (owner: conclusive; ChatGPT x2 and Gemini Deep Research debated, none of their lists is evidence, only seeds). (1) Ask Planner about every water, once, never re-ask an answered phrase (a zero is an answer). (2) One phrasing per intent: 'X lake level' vs 'X lake water level' got the same band in 3,221 of 3,531 pairs, so a second wording adds nothing; rivers get TWO intents (level, flow: Muskegon level 50 vs flow 500 differ), coast gets tide times plus tide chart on beach names (13 'tide chart' rows exist vs 7,999 'tides'). (3) Batches of 1,500 (`tools/kp_upload.py`, 5 batches, 7,302 phrases = 226 AI seeds + all 2,835 queued river names x2), each repeats 21 control phrases of known bands; a control that changes band voids the batch. (4) Status per row: observed band / observed zero / unknown (never overwrite unknown with zero). (5) Country runs after US: GB, CA, AU, IE, NZ with top waters only, one run per country, never summed. (6) Stop rule: if batch 1 returns data (band above 0) for under 5% of the queued river names, Planner cannot see small rivers: stop uploading river names, use Search Console and Wikipedia as tiebreak. (7) Success test: share of queued rivers with a measured row, before 5% / after target 60%. Owner step: the run needs the logged-in Google Ads account in Chrome (Claude does it, no passwords typed). Wikidata for identity and Bing Webmaster export added to the list; Gemini's Trends-anchor method is screening only, not ranking.
DECIDED 2026-11-22 (ChatGPT guest chat, full answer read; docs/handoffs/chatgpt-queue-brief-2026-11-22.md): agreed and to do: (a) unknown is not zero: separate Planner 0 / no row / held-ambiguous (release.py gives all three money 0 today); (b) cap the ad-rate multiplier (Lake Powell bid 13.07 / median 3.11 = 4.2x would dominate) (a guess: cap near 3x); (c) match keywords by state with GNIS, held to a review CSV when ambiguous, zero wrong high bands in 100 checked; (d) lake experiment scorecard at day 14: index rate, impressions per indexed page, clicks, high-band vs band-0 lakes (thresholds are ChatGPT's guesses: 80% indexed, 2x impressions); (e) 25 to 50 a day only after 85% of the last 100 pages are indexed within 28 days. Disagreed: its 15/5/5 mixed daily split (owner ordered pure Keyword Planner; no Search Console cluster data yet to fill the 'proven cluster' slots; revisit after the 2026-12-10 Search Console pull); its claim that the 5% rule double counts demand (it is a ranking weight set 2026-11-06 so 15 gauges on one river do not outrank 250-search creeks, not a demand estimate). Verified on the live site 2026-11-22: about, privacy, contact, methodology, terms return 200 (sources returns 404). The 12,554 world-queue rows with no gauge are keywords with no matched station, not lost waters.
DECIDED 2026-11-21 (owner: 'we are doing the entire world, lakes, oceans and rivers'; Claude and ChatGPT debated, agreed with changes): expand worldwide, order by money. (1) Lakes and reservoirs FIRST: head terms are ~50,000 searches a month (lake powell level, lake mead level, canyon lake, choke canyon, hartwell, kerr) against ~500 for rivers; only 37 lakes are built and 719 USGS lake gauges are not buildable by our code. Competitor lakebrief.com reportedly covers 1,246 US lakes from USGS gauges plus NWS (read by ChatGPT; site returns 403 to bots; the 4,000 visits a month is an unverified Ahrefs estimate). (2) Then coast: named tide and water-level stations, selective wave-buoy pages. No indexed pages for Argo floats, gliders, drifters or model grid cells (doorway risk, would cost the whole site). (3) Then world lakes, rivers and coasts through national agencies, each feed only after its reuse terms are read. (4) Rate: keep 25 a day in total; new country feeds start at 5 a day and go to 10 after 30 clean days; shift the daily mix toward lakes (guess, not a Google limit). (5) A page may exist only if it has a named entity, an official reusable observation, current and historical data, context (unit, datum, source, time), and a distinct search intent; two stations in one harbour do not get two near-identical pages. (6) Cohort gate before more feeds: per feed and category track released, indexed, with impressions, impressions and clicks per indexed page, zero-impression share at 60 and 90 days; never auto-delete. (7) Verify lakebrief cheaply: site:lakebrief.com checks, Ahrefs top pages (is it only the home page?), referring domains, domain age, ten lake pages against Google. STATUS: not built; first job is USGS lake gauges (719) through the existing lake page code. Plan (workflow 2026-11-21, read-only, unverified): the code already builds USGS lakes ('lake' is in BUILDS); the blocker is no data/lakes.csv row and no queue entry, so 'cannot build 719' is a wrong label; 32 of the 719 are already live, ~663 distinct lakes remain, ~580-620 real lakes by name; the money is ~39 gauges at tier 5000 or above (first: Hartwell SC 02187010 50000, Lanier GA 02334400 50000, then Falls, Murray, Thurmond, Allatoona, Green River Lake, Nolin, Barren River, Texoma, Sam Rayburn, Conroe, Belton and others at 5000); Lake Mead, Kerr and Lake Pleasant AZ are not in the 719 (other feeds). Steps: confirm GitHub minute billing and check Powell's 24 h zero; one shared TAILWATER regex (lib/common.py) so tailwater gauges route to river; 00062 and datum fixtures and a USGS-only 'below full pool' check; cadence-aware stale limits and a lake suspect() mark; tools/lake_match.py (LK type, one of 62614/62615/72020/00062 in 30 days, no tailwater, state check, one primary per lake and state, rows queued; first 5 gated practice); rerun world_queue.py for tiers; LAKE_FIRST in release.py (a guess: up to 15 of the 25, total stays 25); Powell Reclamation block (RISE item 508); 7 d and 30 d change and typical-for-date chart. Min lake page: official reading with unit, datum and time, operator pool or full-pool value where a source states it, history, change, nearby inflow gauges; no percent full without a source denominator. One push, one review mark, one passing practice run before any real release. Edge cases: datum mixup, tailwater as lake, duplicate lakes (28 names carry 84 gauges), thin reading-only pages, stuck sensor and daily cadence, wrong-state name match, CI time (~1,200 pages near 39 min, brake at 45).

DATA 2026-11-21 (GA4 read live, 28 d Oct 24 to Nov 20, 510 sessions): Direct 249 (49%, 27% engaged, 11 s; likely outreach clicks, link checkers or bots: unproven), AI Assistant 177 (35%, 45% engaged, 32 s), Organic Search 63 (12%, 65% engaged, 35 s), Referral 13, Social 8. Daily users peaked about Nov 13 (about 60) and fell to 7 on Nov 20: a spike and fade, not growth; the Nov 13 peak matches the outreach emails (guess). Tracking works (average engagement 21 s, not 0). AI assistants send 3x Google visits and engage better than Direct: keep what ChatGPT-style citations need (clear text answers, labelled source and time). Key events 0 and revenue 0: no ad money exists yet, AdSense is the gate.

DECIDED 2026-11-21 (traffic debate, ChatGPT chat 6b010713, GA4 numbers given verbatim): (a) outreach/AI peak then fade = plausible, not proven; (b) 'Direct is mostly bots' = OVERSTATED (Direct also holds untagged email links, bookmarks, missing referrers; GA4 cannot classify bots, only server logs can); (c) 'AI sessions prove citations' = WRONG as stated (attribution is not citation; test by asking the assistants the question and checking whether our page is quoted). Checks to run: GA4 Traffic acquisition by Session source/medium with Date secondary (Nov 12-17 vs 18-20); Direct sessions by landing page, device, country, browser; Explore free-form AI landing pages; GSC Performance Nov 1-5 vs 13-20 for established pages vs new pages (a Google quality problem would show as established pages losing impressions and positions together, indexed count falling without noindex changes, and 14-28 days of decline; none seen yet). Levers ranked: 1 Google organic (63 to 150 sessions/28 d by Dec 20, start with pages at pos 8-20), 2 outreach with UTM-tagged links (measurable referrals by Dec 5, no new big batch before the first one is measured), 3 AI referral (177 to 250 by Dec 20, keep plain labelled text, no tricks). Also: 510 sessions gave 0 key events and $0, so measurement of gauge use and ad revenue is missing; AdSense approval is the gate. Goal is valuable sessions, not 1,000 sessions.

DECIDED 2026-11-21 (river queue audit, owner asked 'did rivers do the dry run', ChatGPT debate in chat 6b010713, agreed): rivers already have a nationwide classification (data/waves.csv: 9,223 gauges = 7,282 queue, 415 live, 1,243 excluded, 283 held-names; ordered by Keyword Planner demand via tools/world_queue.py; 25 a day = about 290 days of queue, NOT ~1,000 as said earlier). No new river discovery sweep now. Do instead: (1) audit the top 300 queued rivers against demand; (2) the 1,243 excluded rows hide real demand, read locally: 546 tiny-basin (e.g. Loxahatchee River at Jupiter FL), 490 short-record (e.g. Little Red River at Heber Springs AR, Russell Fork at Cedarville KY, score 68-70), 206 excluded-name (bypass/floodway channels, correct); Santa Fe River at Graham FL (score 88.4, top of the excluded list) carries reason '70' that is not a reason: find out why; (3) the 283 held-names include the biggest named rivers: Pecos River at Brotherton Rh near Pandale TX (46,027 Wikipedia views), Pecos at Rr 1776 near Grandfalls, Neches at Sh 7 near Pollok, Firehole at Old Faithful YNP, Menominee at McAllister, Alapaha, Cannon at Faribault: held for leftover short tokens (Rh, Sh, Rr). Fix by evidence per row (the USGS site page and the agency name), never by guessing 'Rh = Ranch', in tools/clean_names.py or data/name-fixes.csv, highest wiki views first. Targeted discovery only if the audit shows missing demand. Keep 25 a day.

## Revenue goal (owner 2026-11-10): $1,500 a month by 2027-11-10
Math (assumptions marked): $1,500 / page RPM $5-10 (ASSUMED until AdSense reports ours) = 150,000-300,000 page views a month. Measured 2026-11-10: ~200 sessions a month (ChatGPT 112, direct 60, Google 18), 241 gauge pages, Google position 13.1, 21 clicks in 28 days, AdSense 'Getting ready' (earns $0).
Measured 2026-11-14: GSC weekly impressions 94 / 514 / 1,451 / ~1,260 (weeks of Oct 19 to Nov 9, last one pro-rated from 4 days), clicks ~7 a week, CTR 1.0%, position 17.0; 172 pages earned impressions in the last 7 days; GA4 28 d: direct 171, ChatGPT 143, Google 20 sessions; gauge sessions 24 s engaged; 317 live gauges, 448 pages, releases ~19 gauges per release, about 15 a day (stale: the daily job now releases 25, tools/release.py 25 in sharded.yml) (241 on Nov 10), 7,399 queued. Keyword Planner tiers: 1,476 measured keywords, ~930k searches a month in all. Best guess (owner asked): $1,000 a month needs ~200-250k page views at an assumed $4-5 RPM; at today's position and CTR the full queue (~Dec 2027) gives ~$150-400 a month, so $1,000 needs position 3-5 on most pages (CTR 8-10%); likeliest first $1,000 month: summer 2028 peak season, if AdSense is approved and rankings mature.
Why ChatGPT sends readers (2026-11-14, GA4 60 d + asked ChatGPT in Chrome, owner request): 144 ChatGPT sessions, all in November, 80% mobile, mostly US. Real readers land on gauge pages and stay 30-80 s (Animas at Farmington 14 and Durango 11, Verde, Salt, Squamish, Peace River); Rhine 22 at ~9 s; 24 "ChatGPT" sessions on Pere Marquette carry fbclid, so a ChatGPT link was reshared on Facebook. Asked "Animas River flow in Durango right now, good for rafting?": ChatGPT answered from our page and cited "River Water Levels" first (flow, gauge height, "Rising 4.3% over 6 hours", "much above normal", "above 95% of Nov 14 readings"). Asked why: the page does the interpretation in one place: date-specific percentile, 1/3/6/12/24 h trends, NWS forecast, rain, channel width/depth at a similar flow, upstream gauges; "convenient, not more authoritative than USGS". Its suggestion: run sections with difficulty and runnable ranges beside the reading (American Whitewater). DECIDED 2026-11-14, not built: AW Terms of Service (americanwhitewater.org/?p=800, read 2026-11-14, page dated 2019) allow personal non-commercial use only, bar harvesting by any method, and AW offers no API; no open official source publishes run sections with runnable ranges nationwide, and 'runnable' is a verdict (CLAUDE.md BANNED). A bare link to AW state lists adds no gain.
Why ChatGPT picks us over snoflo.org (2026-11-14, asked ChatGPT in Chrome, Animas at Durango, owner request): ChatGPT's first fetch of snoflo got 4,390 cfs dated Nov 4 (10 days old); a re-fetch got 1,170 cfs; ours gave 1,210 cfs with "2:30 PM MST, 38 minutes old". Its stated reasons: exact observation time and age beside the number, answer on the first screen, each figure labelled measured / our math / forecast, less clutter (snoflo: premium prompts, ads, charts still loading; 641 KB, 41 scripts vs our 248 KB). snoflo also puts the reading in its title, so search snippets show stale numbers, and its robots.txt bars /get_realtime* and /api/. Lesson kept as a rule already in CLAUDE.md: time and age stay beside the headline number. Its idea of a plain JSON of the live reading: /live/<site>.json already exists; nothing built.
GSC 2026-11-15 (28 d to Nov 13, Google lags 2 days): 29 clicks, 3,039 impressions, CTR 0.95%. Impressions by position: top 3 = 8, 4-10 = 201 (1 click), 11-20 = 72, 21+ = 427 of query impressions: clicks need top 3 and we have almost none there. Average position fell 7 to 47 because new pages enter deep, not because old ones dropped. Peace River's 7 clicks were Nov 2-5 (a rain event), then about 1 click a day from many pages. Only page near the top with volume: Rio Grande at Albuquerque, position ~8.5, 110 impressions on 'rio grande water level albuquerque (today)', 1 click (title test reads 12-07). 'at-mio-16l' queries (an electronics part) hit the Au Sable at Mio page by name: harmless.
FREEZE 2026-11-15 to 2026-11-29 (owner OK, 'I think I'm over engineering'): no new rules, gates, checks, hooks or jobs. Only: (a) the daily job's one fix a day from tools/fix_list.py; (b) lead sentences where a block lacks one (home page stays as it was: owner 2026-11-15, a summary goes stale) (computed from data, no prose; the 'about this water' paragraph is dropped); (c) reading results: AdSense 11-26, Search Console 11-25, outreach 12-11. A real wrong-number bug is always fixed.
AdSense reapply prep (owner reapplies 2026-11-26; review read 2026-11-15 of 22 live pages, verdict 'borderline, likely reject again'). Fix only, never cut (CLAUDE.md). DONE 11-15: (1) no ads on contact, terms, privacy, disclaimer; (2) all-Unknown table columns said once; (3) each source credit once per page. NEXT: (4) one plain lead sentence per block where missing; (5) a sourced 'about this water' paragraph from agency facts (USGS site description, NID dam, PAD-US access), never invented prose; (6) grow the one-gauge states AL, IL, ND, NV in the release order; (7) home 'rivers today' summary; (8) About page: owner gives two lines (who, why). Search Console messages read 2026-11-15: Datasets 'Missing field license' (Nov 12): FIXED, every Dataset names its source's terms page, check.py holds one without; the rest are the Oct-Nov noindex-era indexing notices and the Analytics link.
Thin pages (owner 2026-11-15 'never push out thin pages again'), guards in order: check.py page rules and hub text FLOOR; info gain vs the top competitor for lakes and coasts (release); world ship test (lib/world.py); NEW thin-kind gate (tools/open_wave.py held_kinds): Google's own verdict, a kind with 3+ pages 'Crawled - currently not indexed' and 15%+ of its judged pages gets no new pages (release and open) until improved; and request-indexing never re-asks a declined page. Today: 1 declined (England Colwick), no kind held.
Pages, counted 2026-11-15 from the live sitemap (the only count to quote): 445 open to Google = 317 US gauge pages (225 river, 57 coast, 35 lake) + 44 world gauge pages + 78 hubs (27 river, 38 state, 12 country/state, 1 lakes) + 6 site pages. Release pace, measured from data/index-allow.json commits: 9 releases in the 12 days 11-03 to 11-14 (none on 11-07, 11-08, 11-10), 12 to 25 gauges each, 175 in all = about 15 a day, not 25 (25 is the cap; failing new pages go back to the queue). At 15 a day about 5,500 more by 2027-11, not 9,000. The '724 pages' in the cloud check log is the test build's file count, not pages in Google. Google, URL Inspection of all 445 on 2026-11-15: 343 indexed, 84 'Excluded by noindex' (Google's view from the noindex era; 5 sampled live carry no noindex; validation result due 11-25), 14 discovered not indexed, 3 crawled not indexed, 1 unknown (/river/russian-river/). Each needs about 20-30 views a month (today under 1): ranking, not page count, decides it.

## Limits (dates at 25 a day)
1. USGS 1,000 calls/h: history weekly in 168 slots, 325 calls/h at 9,100; a 429 stops history for that run.
2. Data cache: 16 MB at 97 gauges, about 1.5 GB at 9,100. GitHub evicts oldest; no pause rule.
6. Cloudflare 20,000 files, 3 per gauge: Workers Paid (100,000) or one shared embed page. Before 6,000, about July 2027. 14,000 gauges is 42,000 files.
7. Release pauses over 70% of plan files, a refresh over 45 min, or 10 Worker stops a day on the free plan; each holds growth, never a page.
8. World Worker refresh: station names in one URL break near 150 gauges; a whole-feed reply (786 stations, about 650 KB) risks the 10 ms CPU. Before 150: per-feed copies from the hourly build, or Workers Paid. Each country adds one feed call per poll.
9. PARTLY DONE 2026-11-11: world feeds spread across shards, PEGELONLINE bulk current reading; the 8-day history is still one call per station (about 6,000 an hour at 6,000 world gauges); split the station list near 500 stations on one page.
10. KV: USGS at most 480 writes a day plus world 96, of 1,000 shared.
11. `_redirects`: rules after the first dynamic one count against 100; single-gauge forwards live in `single-rivers.json` plus the Worker. Link budgets: home 300, state 700.
12. NOAA CO-OPS: 4 calls per coast per hour, no published limit (300 coasts = 28,800 a day): watch 429s. RISE and CDEC publish no limit.

## PLAN 2026-11-25 BUILD ORDER FOR "A SITE PEOPLE RETURN TO" (owner: "plan how to build it all as we grow"; guess-labelled, to debate with ChatGPT before step 3)
Goal: money. Google fell 95% on 11-20 (cause unproven, likely core-update demotion of pages that restate one agency number). Fix = own data, a tool, a reason to return. Not more ranking pages (25 a day stays).
Order = cheapest first, each step judged Helpful / Gain / HCU, each ships behind a fixture and the phone-shot rule.
0. MEASURE (no build, now): daily GSC series on 11-26 and 12-03 (`tools/gsc_read.py`); owner opens Search Console Manual actions and Security issues (no key can read them); a fixed list of 50 queries tracked daily by position (one script, cloud). Decision rule: pages with history or comparison recover first = keep building step 1; nothing moves by 12-03 = still build, but stop judging by Google and judge by Bing, direct, embeds.
1. OWN DATA ON EXISTING PAGES (biggest gain per byte, data already in cache): a "this date in past years" chart and a "same water, this year vs last" line on every gauge and lake page, with the answer sentence first. Edge: no history = block hidden and named in the gaps line, never blank; a year with missing days is drawn with the gap; a unit change between years is held. Scale: bytes per page under MAX_GZ (inline SVG, no script); 14,000 pages x chart = measure at 500 pages first.
2. FINISH /map/ (already built, noindex): phone shots read, add '/map/' to data/reviewed.json 'passed', then search box and filter, then region split before 2,000 dots (JSON about 60 bytes a dot). Edge: stale dot greyed with real age, Unknown never green; map and page numbers come from one source with a build check; Cloudflare files and 10 ms CPU unchanged (static).
3. COMPARE (new information, own page type only if it carries data of its own): upstream/downstream pairs and nearby lakes with the reading side by side, on the existing pages first; a separate page only for a pair people search. Edge: doorway rule in check.py census; links only to built indexable pages.
4. DISTRIBUTION: embeds already live (oEmbed); the ask is links, not pages: hand-checked outreach to lake associations, marinas, local news (the owner approves each batch; sign Michael). Edge: no forced follow links, the credit line stays visible.
5. RETURN LOOP: saved places in the browser (localStorage, no sign-in, no server cost), then alerts only after a reliability test (a feed down is Unknown, never "all clear"). Edge: localStorage blocked = page works without it; alert email needs a sender and consent, owner step.
6. MONEY LAYER: Adsterra live check 11-25; AdSense reapply 11-26 is the owner's call (my advice: wait until steps 1 and 2 are live, a reviewer sees the page as it is); no new network until traffic exists.
Growth gates (as pages grow): 25 a day; raise only when GSC indexed share of the last 28 days is 80% or more and USGS gives no 429s; Cloudflare files self-pause at 14,000 (paid plan, owner step); each new block states its cost at 14,000 pages before build.
Not building: more doorway pages, a login system, a newsletter without real content, deleting the 520 pages.
CORRECTION 2026-11-25 (read the live page /lake/medina-lake/, 121 KB, 38 headings): step 1 above is MOSTLY BUILT already (past-years comparison, 'same 50 days in earlier years', historic crests, dam, Drought Monitor, E. coli, ramps, fish, camera, weather, radar). So the 'thin restater' read is weak: a rich page at position 6.9 vanished with the rest. Cause now: UNKNOWN. Candidates, untested: (a) core-update demotion of a young domain with no links/brand; (b) Google prefers the agencies for 'X water level' (a SERP change, check live results daily); (c) a page-weight or UX signal (121 KB html, many blocks, ad scripts). Step 1 therefore shrinks to 'check coverage gaps (waters with no history), no new chart'. Real levers left: links and a brand/return reason (steps 2, 4, 5), plus the 12-03 read.
DEBATED 2026-11-25 WITH CHATGPT on the build workflow (packet docs/chatgpt-prompts/2026-11-25-build-workflow.md; chat 6b070a4b-b2e0-83e8-b8e7-529a90c4419f; answer read to its last line 'Latest response'). AGREED (adopted): (1) research and audits run in PARALLEL read-only, code runs one writer per file, review and release sequential; (2) outreach starts now, not after the map; (3) saved places after the map proves use; alerts and newsletter cut for now; (4) tracking budget $0: daily GSC snapshots + Bing Webmaster (current REST API) + 15-20 manual Google queries a week, no paid rank API; (5) fix real history gaps, no new chart type; (6) map scope by performance test (clustering, viewport loading), not the arbitrary 2,000 dots; (7) add an ad-readiness audit (AdSense rejection reason, Adsterra) and a 30-second phone-experience audit; (8) release with a recorded change list and rollback (Undo step exists); (9) capacity audit before growth (20,000 files: 14,000 pages x 1 file leaves 6,000 for assets, sitemaps, data; we measured 3.5 files a gauge, so the 14,000-file self-pause stands).
DISAGREED: (a) 'freeze new pages while auditing': NO, owner decided 25 a day 11-24; pages cost nothing while the queue feeds Bing, and a pause does not diagnose anything; tripwire (indexed share under 80% or USGS 429s) stays. (b) 'December 3 is too early': PARTLY AGREED, see next line.

ChatGPT's 'ended 24 Nov 06:03 PT, December started 07:02 PT, active 11-25' is UNVERIFIED in its times (the page gives dates only). ## SPAIN 50 WORKFLOW (owner 2026-11-26: "plan the workflow then deploy 50 pages")
Order: A map (2 read-only agents in parallel: build/publish path; every gate that would fail or silently skip a Spanish page) > B build (sequential, one writer: collector + cloud wiring; pages + sources row + sitemap/indexing; gates + fixtures + edge tests) > C review (2 read-only in parallel: truth/code; Spanish reader + HCU + EU consent) > D one fix agent. No push inside. Then the session: independent check of the fix diff, review mark, push to check with /es/ noindex, cloud phone shots read by a non-builder, reviewed.json, push, read the live pages.
Cost: about 7 agents, roughly 1M tokens. Cloud: the 10.4 MB zip is fetched only when its Last-Modified changes (weekly), not on each hourly refresh. At 375 reservoirs the same one download covers all.
Edge cases: the list under "OWNER 2026-11-26" above; each is done only when a named test passes.
- LIVE 2026-11-27 UTC: /es/ + 50 reservoir pages open. Read on the live site: 50 of 50 linked pages HTTP 200, lang="es", no noindex, no ad code; sitemap has 51 /es/ addresses; home links /es/. Cloud mdb-export proven ("ok es 50 reservoirs, newest 2026-11-24"). Independent shot read PASS (run 45935497961). Reread Search Console for /es/ at 2026-12-04 and 12-25.
- Open: no Spanish consent message or privacy page (Analytics runs consent-denied there, may send cookieless pings: UNVERIFIED live); first next-week file update not yet seen; minor polish from the shot read (date repeated per hub row, hm3 column wraps).
